- **Guest G2 External/lifecycle contract:** `api.ts` requires server-derived `Conversation.hasExternalMembers`, `ChatMessage.authorExternal`/`authorRemoved`, and `RosterMember.external`/`removed`; no client infers guest status from names, email domains, or current membership. `ChannelList` and the active header render their container External/外部 badge directly from `hasExternalMembers` without a channel-roster fan-out. `MessageRow` preserves historical authorship and adds External plus Removed/已移除 only from the author booleans; the settings roster does the same from its person booleans. Optimistic rows render no lifecycle badge until the server projection reconciles them.
- **Guest G2 release contract:** the admin guest console owns invite/re-invite,
  disable/enable/extend, profile CRUD/assignment, and the seven-app profile
  matrix while consuming the backend's frozen camelCase payloads. Guest detail
  loads are generation-scoped and invalidated on close or current-request
  failure, so a deferred response cannot reopen/overwrite another guest and a
  failed fetch cannot leave a backdrop-only modal. New profiles choose a named domain
  from the `guests.view`/`guests.manage`-scoped reachable-domain endpoint; edit
  keeps that ownership domain locked. The guest
  shell remains fail-closed even while capabilities are absent/errored, exposes
  only Chat plus entitlement-backed Meet, redirects every other app route to
  `/chat`, and removes member actions in both empty and
  non-empty Chat. Exact-email member
  preview is bound to the current channel and normalized email so stale
  responses cannot add a guest. The combined backend lifecycle/EventSource
  tracer plus the admin/chat/layout component suites are the G2 release gate;
  `feature.guest_accounts` remains seeded OFF after the gate.
- **Guest invite terminal-lifecycle release contract (V148):** the existing guest detail sheet renders one actionable current-invitation region and a read-only latest-10 history with exact decimal-string ids and Pending/Expired/Accepted/Superseded/Revoked/Historical outcome unavailable labels. Creator attribution is nullable and displayed as unavailable rather than inferred. Revoke sends the inspected id as compare-and-set input and refreshes on 404/409. Both the guest console and Channel Settings surface treat `invite_delivery_unconfirmed` as a committed-but-uncertain state: they show the exact id, never automatically replay create, offer refresh, and route an explicit replacement through `reinviteGuest`. en/de/zh copy and focused component/API tests pin these states.
- **`MessageRow.svelte` / `Composer.svelte`** (A3 QoL Task 15, on top of A1/A2's original build): top-level row actions are react + reply + a **⋯ overflow menu** (`role="menu"`, reusing the react-popover dismiss pattern) holding pin (channel: admin-gated via `canModerate`; DM/group-DM: any member — reads `chatStore.conversations`' own `kind`, no new prop), save (personal bookmark, self-scoped), translate (`feature.assist`-gated via `$effectiveCaps`, renders inline with a "Show original" toggle, never replacing the body), edit, delete. **Read receipts (DES-010)**: computed entirely from existing store state (`chatStore.selfUserId`/`messagesByChannel`/`peerReadSeq`) on the caller's own LATEST message only — a DM tick once the peer's watermark passes, a channel "Read by N" caption fetched via `chatApi.getChannelReaders` on render. **Voice**: `Composer`'s mic button drives `MediaRecorder`/`getUserMedia` (click-to-toggle on every pointer type — hold-to-record on coarse pointers is a documented v1 cut, not built), landing the clip through the EXISTING attachment/chunk-upload pipeline and sending via the new `chatStore.sendVoice(durationMs, parentId)`; playback is a plain `<audio controls>` off the already-resolved attachment content URL. **Polls**: `msgType==='poll'` renders a `fieldset`/`radiogroup` (or checkbox group when `multi`) from `chatStore.pollResultsByPollId`, tallies+voter names (fixed row footprint — a clipped one-line voter list so a growing vote count never changes height), Close offered to the creator (`isMine`, since the poll's anchor-message `authorId` IS the creator — no `createdBy` field exists on `chat_poll_results` to duplicate) or `canModerate`. **Unfurl card**: a fixed ~88px card off `message.payload.preview`, collapsible, image via `chatApi.unfurlImageUrl`. **Send-later**: a split-button beside Send (quick presets + a custom-datetime dialog showing the resolved IANA timezone explicitly) plus an "N scheduled" chip opening a cancel-only queue where a failed row renders with a distinct danger badge + reason + the still-readable body. `MessageList.svelte`/`ThreadPane.svelte` both forward their own `roster` prop straight through to `MessageRow` (one-line addition each) purely for poll voter-name resolution — no other behavior change. The Go route (`POST /chat/channels/{id}/messages`) gained optional `msgType`/`payload` fields to actually reach Task 6's `chat_message_send` seam for voice (see `internal/chat/AGENTS.md`'s "Voice + msg_type/payload send seam" section — that doc owns the Go/reducer side, this one owns everything above). **Per-genre split (whole-pipeline review arch #9):** the poll/call/attachment body renderers described above now live in their own components — `MessageBodyPoll.svelte` (poll render+vote+close), `MessageBodyCall.svelte` (the call card), `MessageAttachments.svelte` (voice player + image/file chips + the lightbox `Dialog`, bundled together because all three ride the SAME node-meta→content-blob resolution pipeline, incl. the module-scope `withFetchSlot` concurrency gate that moved with it) — `MessageRow.svelte` keeps the dispatch chain (`isDeleted`/`editing`/`isPoll`/`isCall`/else), the ordinary text/markdown/translation/unfurl body, and every row-level action (react/reply/pin/save/translate/edit/delete, read receipts). The sanitizing markdown pipeline (`renderChatMarkdown` immediately followed by `{@html}`, XSS-load-bearing) stayed exactly where it was — untouched, still in `MessageRow.svelte`. Tests: `MessageBodyPoll.test.ts`/`MessageBodyCall.test.ts`/`MessageAttachments.test.ts` carry the exhaustive per-genre coverage moved out of `MessageRow.test.ts`, which keeps one wiring-smoke test per genre plus everything dispatch-chain-specific (e.g. soft-delete blanking).
- **Self-identity (whole-pipeline review UX #17, closed):** `/auth/me` now projects `user_id` (authserver `session.go:Me`, straight off the validated JWT claims, R2 — never a request field); the webmail `AuthUser`/`user` store (`$lib/stores/auth.ts`) carries it as `userId`. `ChatApp.svelte`'s `resolveSelfUserId` reads `$user.userId` directly and sets `chatStore.selfUserId` from it — no roster fetch, no displayName comparison. The prior displayName-matching-against-the-first-DM-roster hack (wrong in duplicate-displayName orgs — DM titles, "mine" highlights, self read-tick filtering) is kept ONLY as a fallback for an old gateway that hasn't shipped the `user_id` field yet. Server-side `p_user_id` from the session was always the real authorization boundary regardless of this client-side identity. The New-DM dialog still takes raw comma-separated user ids (same shape as Drive's `MembersSheet` numeric-id gap — no email→userId directory-lookup route on the chat surface; unrelated to this fix).
- **Panels/views/settings (A3 QoL Task 16, pure webmail — no Go/reducer change).** `ChatApp` gained a `mainView` state machine (`'conversation'|'saved'|'threads'|'search'`) that swaps what renders BESIDE `ChannelList` in the main pane; selecting a real channel always resets it to `'conversation'`. Four new presentational components, each a one-shot `chatApi` load (no live-push arm — same "no live fetch for invisible state" posture DES-010 already accepts):
  - **`PinsPanel.svelte`** — a channel-scoped SIDE panel (not a main-view swap) reusing the exact same `.chat-app__thread-region` slide-in/mobile-overlay `ThreadPane` uses; `ChatApp` keeps the two mutually exclusive (opening one closes the other). Lists `chatApi.listPins`, each row an inline unpin action.
  - **`SavedView.svelte`** / **`MyThreadsView.svelte`** — main-view swaps for the ChannelList's two new FIXED rows ("Saved"/"Threads", Slack's "Later"/"Threads" placement, `Bookmark`/`MessageSquare` icons) added above the regular conversation list in `ChannelList.svelte`; the Threads row carries an aggregate unread badge (`chat_my_threads`' `unreadCount`, summed — `ChannelList` fetches this itself, refreshed on the same `conversations`-change effect its own `listMembers`/`getPresence` calls already ride, no new poll timer). Both resolve a channel's display NAME off `chatStore.conversations` only — a DM/group_dm row falls back to the generic "Direct message" label rather than a per-row roster resolve (ponytail, documented inline; `ChannelList`'s own `resolveDmTitles` is where that real resolution is worth the round trip).
  - **`SearchView.svelte`** — the two entry points the plan calls for: `ChannelList`'s new header search `Input` (global, Enter-submits, no live-as-you-type) and the conversation header's search `IconButton` (pre-scoped to the open channel via `initialChannelId`, with a "This channel only" `Checkbox` to broaden without retyping). Results group by channel — `chat_search` returns newest-first on the global id (see `internal/chat/AGENTS.md`), so grouping by first-occurrence order sorts groups "most-recently-active channel first" for free, no extra sort. `Load more` pages via `before` (the last-seen `messageId`).
  - **`ChannelSettingsSheet.svelte`** — the gear `IconButton` in the conversation header. One sheet, two authority levels: the per-channel `notifLevel` select is the CALLER'S OWN mute preference (every member may set it) and always renders; the `postPermission`/`broadcastPermission` selects (Task 8) are channel-wide owner/admin policy and render only when `canManage && kind==='channel'` — a plain member sees the notif control plus a one-line explanatory note instead of a disabled-and-confusing select. Saving is two independent calls (`chatApi.setChannelNotif` always-attempted-if-changed; `chatApi.updateChannel({postPermission, broadcastPermission})` only when `canManage`), then `chatStore.loadConversations()` refreshes the fields everywhere they're read. Guest G2 also threads the already-fetched active roster into this sheet for lifecycle badges and gives non-guest channel managers an exact-email preview. `activeGuest` discloses join-time versus full-history access before calling the existing `addMember`; `inviteRequired` never guesses an identity — `guests.manage` callers reuse `$lib/admin/guests`' direct invite, while ordinary members reuse the approval-backed G1 `InviteGuestButton`. A direct invite does not auto-grant the channel: the copy requires adding the guest after acceptance.
  - **Guest invite entry (Guest G1 Task 7, consumed by G2):** `InviteGuestButton` stays the ordinary-member branch after an exact-email preview returns `inviteRequired`. It remains visible-but-disabled with an explanatory tooltip when guest accounts are on but approval is off, opens a kit dialog when both effective bits are true, and strictly posts `{invitedEmail,message}` to `/api/me/guest-invites`. Success/error are inline and localized in en/de/zh; guest callers and sessions with guest accounts OFF never see the exact-email entry.
  - All four rows/click-throughs funnel into ONE handler, `ChatApp.jumpTo(channelId, seq)` — sets `mainView='conversation'` and calls `chatStore.openChannelAt` (Task 10's jump-to-message substrate), matching the plan's "all click-throughs → openChannelAt."
  - **Live fields this task exposed for the first time on the client**: `api.ts`'s `Conversation` type gained `notifLevel`/`postPermission`/`broadcastPermission` (sent by `chat_conversation_list` since Tasks 7/8, but never typed until the settings sheet needed to read them) and `updateChannel`'s parameter type widened to accept the two permission fields (the route has taken them since Task 8; the client type didn't). `ChatApp`'s `composerDisabled` — previously hard-coded `false` with a "nothing to gate on yet" ponytail comment — now reads `postPermission` for real.
  - **Mute consequence** (`ChannelList.svelte`): a conversation's own `notifLevel` drives BOTH badge visibility (`'none'` suppresses it outright; `'mentions'` shows it only for an actual mention, never a plain unread count; `'all'`/absent behaves as before) and a `BellOff` glyph + caption-color row name. This is the per-channel vocabulary (`'all'|'mentions'|'none'`) — NOT the global `user_notification_prefs.chat_notifications` (`'all'|'mentions'|'off'`), see `internal/chat/AGENTS.md`'s "two different vocabularies" note.
- Tests: `src/lib/components/chat/ChannelList.test.ts`, `src/lib/components/chat/ChatApp.test.ts`, `PinsPanel.test.ts`, `SavedView.test.ts`, `MyThreadsView.test.ts`, `SearchView.test.ts`, `ChannelSettingsSheet.test.ts`.
- **Meet↔Chat bridge UX (pipeline-review fixes, webmail-only, consuming already-landed backend keys).** `api.ts`'s `Conversation` gains `meetingId?: number | null` (`chat_conversation_list` has sent it since backend V131 — the meeting whose bridged chat this channel is) — `ChannelList.svelte`'s row icon renders `Video` + the `--accent` color (`.cl__icon--meeting`) instead of the generic `MessageCircle`/`Users` whenever `meetingId != null`, so a meeting-bridged channel reads as a call, not an unlabeled ghost channel with permanent unread. **The chat→meet huddle bridge had no SENDER**: `meeting_create` accepts `chatChannelId` (huddle: post a `'call'` card into the channel) and `MessageRow` already rendered the receiver-side call card, but nothing in webmail ever posted one — `ChatApp.svelte`'s conversation header gains a "Start call" `IconButton` (`Video` icon, next to Search/Pins/Settings), gated on `enabledApps.includes('meet')`, calling `meetApi.createMeeting({ title: activeTitle, chatChannelId: conv.channelId })` then `goto('/meet?slug=' + created.slug)` (design #14's canonical deep-link shape). **`MessageRow.svelte`'s call card** now hides the Join button (replaced by a "Meeting unavailable" caption) whenever `'meet'` is absent from `$effectiveCaps.enabledApps` — with `feature.meet` OFF, Join used to 404 code-less and dead-end into the Mail app via the layout guard's silent bounce to `$firstEnabledApp`, with zero explanation. New i18n: `chat.list.meetingChannel`, `chat.header.startCall`, `chat.call.unavailable` (en/de/zh). Tests: `ChannelList.test.ts` (meeting icon render), `ChatApp.test.ts` (Start-call visibility/gating/click), `MessageRow.test.ts` (meet-off Join guard).
- **Compliance minimum (Chat A4a Task 13, deliberately minimal per the plan).** A channel-header **WORM badge** (`ShieldCheck`, icon+tooltip) renders when `conversation.classification === 'gxp'`, and a separate **retention indicator** (`Clock`, icon+tooltip) whenever `retentionDays` is non-null — both read from `api.ts`'s `Conversation` type, which gained `classification`/`retentionDays` this task (`chat_conversation_list` has sent both since Task 8; no client type declared them until now, same pattern as `postPermission`/`broadcastPermission` before it). `retentionNotice(classification, retentionDays)` is single-homed in `store.svelte.ts` so the header tooltip and `ChannelSettingsSheet`'s new read-only Retention row can never diverge on the pinned destroy-ceiling-vs-WORM-floor wording (a swap reads as the opposite of what the knob does and destroys customer data — see `internal/chat/AGENTS.md`'s vocabulary block). **`legalHold` is deliberately never projected to end users** (tipping-off — a custodian must not learn their own channel is under hold) and **`last_purged_seq` is deliberately never projected** (provenance, not a client-visible cursor) — both decisions, not oversights. `chatErrorReason`/`chatErrorMessage` map **BZ193** → `chat.error.wormImmutable`, **BZ196** → `chat.error.rateLimited` (any other code passes the server's own `detail` through). Fixed two real gaps this task's design review found: `MessageRow.saveEdit`/`doDelete` used to `await` the store with no `try/catch` (the store re-throws) — a gxp channel's BZ193 used to leave the edit box open with zero signal and a rejected delete raised silently; both now `try/catch` + `showToast(chatErrorMessage(err), {variant:'danger'})`, and the edit box only ever clears on the success path. `#doSend`'s catch now stamps `errorCode` from a `ChatError` onto the failed row, so a BZ196-rate-limited send's Retry button finally shows a reason. i18n `chat.compliance.*`/`chat.error.*` (en/de/zh), retention wording byte-identical to `admin.chatCompliance.retention.destroyCeiling`/`wormFloor`.

---

### Meet (`src/routes/meet/+page.svelte`, B1 core + B2 recording)

Capability-gated the same way as mail/calendar/contacts/tasks/drive/chat (the `appForPath` route guard in `+layout.svelte`, `'meet'` in `AppId`/`APP_ORDER`/`APP_PATHS`/`RAIL_APPS`), behind `feature.meet` (DEFAULT OFF); recording controls additionally gate on `feature.meet_recording` (DEFAULT OFF, B2).

- **`MeetApp.svelte`** (`src/lib/components/meet/`, 704 lines — down from 1307, arch review #A12) owns the in-meeting shell: prejoin screen, gallery/speaker layout, `ControlsBar`, bridged in-meeting chat (a meeting's chat IS a `chat_channels` row — the Chat components above render inline, no separate meet-chat implementation), self-hosted WASM background blur (probe-gated via a codec/HW try-encode probe, `@livekit/track-processors`, the one CSP delta — see the CSP note at the top of this file). **State ownership (`$lib/meet/store.svelte.ts`, `MeetStore`, arch review #A12):** the meeting/view/push slice — `view`/`meeting`/`room`/`errorMessage`/`meetings`/`sidePanel`/`whiteboardOpen`/`knockCreds`/`openGeneration`/the recording+captions+breakout+moveInterstitial fields, the view-FSM transitions (`openMeeting`/`openBySlug`/`openEndedMeeting`/`declinePreJoin`/`handlePreJoin`/`handleKnockAdmitted`/`connectRoom`/`leave`/`teardownRoom`/`teardownLiveRoom`/`loadMeetings`), and the ~240-line `MeetEvent` push-reconciliation handler (`applyEvent`, the `ChatStore.applyEvent` precedent) — lives in `MeetStore`, a real rune class MeetApp reads from (`const store = new MeetStore()`, instantiated fresh per mount, NOT a module singleton like `chatStore`/`driveLocks` — the original component-local `$state` reset to defaults on every mount, and a shared singleton would silently change that). `MeetStore implements MeetActionHost` directly (its fields ARE the interface — no accessor facade), fixing the PRIOR inverted-ownership shape (`MeetActionHost` mutating MeetApp's own component `$state` through get/set accessors, vs every other app where the store owns state and the component reads). `$lib/meet/actions.svelte.ts` (`MeetActions`, whole-pipeline review arch #9) is unchanged by this — `new MeetActions(this)` in `MeetStore`'s own constructor needs zero edits to `actions.svelte.ts` or its test, since the interface shape didn't change, only who implements it. What stayed in MeetApp.svelte (component-lifecycle territory — `$effect`s constructed in a class constructor would throw `effect_orphan` when a test instantiates `MeetStore` standalone): DOM refs + the focus-restore/disconnect/slug-deep-link `$effect`s, the `breakoutAssignments` meeting-reseed `$effect`, template-only `$derived` projections (`sheetParticipants`/`hasGuestParticipant`/`bannedList`/`chatEnabled`), the landing-page create/join form (`createTitle`/`joinLinkInput`/`createMeeting`/`joinByLink`/`extractSlug`), `galleryView`/`currentTheme`, and the `doStartRecording`/…/`doSetRole`/`doReAdmit` thin call-throughs onto `store.actions.*` (unchanged shape from arch #9, just reached via `store.actions` instead of a local `actions` instance). The WS subscription itself (`subscribeEvent('MeetEvent', (evt) => store.applyEvent(evt))`) also stays component-side, the `ChatApp`/`chatStore.applyEvent` precedent. Tests: `actions.svelte.test.ts` (unit, plain-object host, unchanged); `store.svelte.test.ts` (new — the three structural pins that moved with their functions: F3 teardownLiveRoom-first, the loadMeetings showToast pin, D4 teardownLiveRoom field-clearing); `MeetApp.test.ts` keeps its full behavioral suite UNCHANGED and green (347 tests — the WS/breakout/recording scenarios exercise `store.applyEvent` transitively through the same `subscribeEvent` mock, proving the extraction is behavior-preserving) plus its own narrowed structural pins (`createMeeting`, which stayed inline; the four `do*` handlers as thin call-throughs onto `store.actions.*`).
- **Guest G3 Meet shell:** authenticated guest sessions use named `meet` EventSource frames and the embedded Meet chat uses named `chat` EventSource frames; members retain the bidirectional JMAP WebSocket. Reconnect reconciles the current meeting and Chat backlog. A guest BZ215 on join alone starts an authenticated slug-bound knock; member BZ215 remains an error and member BZ187 retains the existing knock behavior. Create is hidden unless the session's fail-closed guest policy carries `allowMeetCreate`. Participant tiles and the participant sheet render External only from the signed LiveKit `setjoin.external=true` attribute (`participantAttributes.ts`), never from identity prefixes; anonymous `g:` identities keep Guest. The existing recording-in-progress interstitial and non-dismissible live banner are explicitly pinned for authenticated guests, including admission racing a recording state change.
- **B2 additions (Task 10, on top of the B1 shell):**
  - **Consent banner** — non-dismissible, driven ONLY by server state (`meeting_get`'s live recording state via `MeetEvent`/`recording_state` pushes — never local/optimistic state; a client that missed the push still sees the truth on its next poll/reconnect). Maps `requested|active|stopping` → visually "recording ON" (the truthful lifecycle: a stop shows `stopping…` immediately, `stopped` only lands once `egress_ended` actually confirms it — never a premature "recording off" the moment a host clicks Stop).
  - **Join interstitials** — both the member and the guest-lobby belt show the SAME truthful recording-state banner before granting a live view of the room, so nobody joins (or is admitted into) a recording mid-flight without warning; this is the disable-recording-when-guest-present invariant's CLIENT-side mirror of the server's `FOR UPDATE`-enforced BZ199 (the client never trusts its own copy — a race is still resolved server-side, this is UX only).
  - **Recording controls** (`ControlsBar`) — host/cohost-only start/stop button; in-flight states disable the confirm control and show an in-progress chip/`stopping…` label rather than a spinner-less no-op; `egressBusy` (503, distinguishable RFC7807 `code`) renders its own toast copy, not the generic "could not start" message.
  - **Recordings list w/ playback** — a per-meeting list (from `meeting_get`'s `recordings[]`, host/cohost + post-end attendee visible only, `meeting_attendee_role`-gated server-side) with inline playback off the Drive content route (`GET /api/drive/versions/{id}/content`, the same Range-streaming path any Drive file uses — no separate meet-recording byte path).
  - **Attendance panel + CSV** (the third B1-inherited-by-name item) — reads `meeting_get`'s `attendance[]` (host/cohost + post-end, same gate as recordings), renders a table, and exports CSV client-side (no server export route — the array is already small and host-only).
  - **Always-mounted live region + interstitial focus move** (design review D-5): the recording-state announcement region is mounted unconditionally (not created/destroyed with the banner) so a screen reader never misses a state flip that happens between two DOM-diff passes; opening a join interstitial moves focus into it.
  - i18n: full en/de/zh triads for every string above, `formatDateTime` for every timestamp, a never-blank-name fallback for a recording's display name.
- **Unauthenticated guest join page** (B1) — the one public Meet surface; guests get media + reactions only, no chat rail (no `users` row — guest-accounts program), no recording controls (host/cohost only) and no recordings-list access (arm 3 keys on `users` rows, guests have none — `docs/features.md`'s Meet entry).
- **ponytail gap**: no numeric self-user-id exposed to the client beyond what B1 already resolves (same shape as Drive's `MembersSheet`/Chat's New-DM gap) — not new to B2.
- Tests: component tests for the consent banner (server-state-only, never local-state regression), the interstitial gate, and the recording controls' in-flight/egressBusy states; e2e coverage rides the existing Meet B1 suite plus the B2 additions above.
- Full server-side contract + the B2 honesty ledger (live Valkey+Egress round-trip not run in this sandbox): `mailserver/internal/meet/AGENTS.md`.
- **Meet↔Chat bridge UX (pipeline-review fixes, webmail-only).** `MeetApp.svelte`'s `doEnd`/`doLock`/`doKick`/`doSetRole`/`createMeeting`/`loadMeetings` used to catch failures into `errorMessage`, a `$state` that only ever renders when `view==='error'` — none of those six handlers set that view, so a failed host-control action (or a failed create/list) vanished with zero UI feedback. All six now `showToast(errText(e))`, the SAME idiom the recording/captions handlers already used. **Huddle non-member chat gating (V131 consequence)**: `api.ts`'s `Meeting` gains `chatChannelMember?: boolean` (`meeting_get` — whether the caller is actually a member of `chatChannelId`; `normalizeMeeting` defaults it `true` so a pre-V131 server response, where every bound channel WAS auto-join, behaves unchanged) — `MeetApp.svelte`'s chat-panel/Poll gates (`onOpenChat`/`onOpenPoll`/the `sidePanel==='chat'` render) now additionally require `meeting.chatChannelMember`, so a participant who joined a huddle bound to a pre-existing channel they are NOT a member of (Slack semantics: meeting only, never the channel's history) never sees an empty/erroring chat panel. **`MeetChatPanel.svelte`** never called `chatStore.loadConversations()` — `MessageList`'s `markReadIfEligible` looks the channel up in `chatStore.conversations` before marking anything read, and `chatStore.openChannel()` alone never populates that array, so a message read live in-meeting was never actually marked read (a phantom unread left behind once the meeting ended); fixed with an `onMount` call. Tests: `MeetApp.test.ts` (toast-on-failure for all six handlers, behavioral for lock/end/createMeeting + a structural regex pin for all six; the `chatChannelMember` gating), `MeetChatPanel.test.ts` (`loadConversations` called on mount), `api.test.ts` (`chatChannelMember` default/pass-through).
- **B1 live-gate ledger fixes (2026-07-16, webmail-only, three own commits).** The B1 webmail-UI authority-flow run ledgered three UI gaps in `TODOS.md`'s Meet B1 section, since fixed. **Kicked member freeze:** `notify_meet_state`'s fan-out excludes the just-kicked identity itself (`meeting_kick` stamps `left_at` before the `PERFORM` runs, and the fan-out `SELECT` filters `left_at IS NULL`) — the kicked member's own client never sees the `participant_kicked` push, so the only signal it gets is LiveKit's own `Disconnected` event with reason `PARTICIPANT_REMOVED` (`room.svelte.ts`'s `disconnectReason` field existed for exactly this but had no consumer). `MeetApp.svelte`'s pre-existing breakout-disconnect `$effect` now checks `disconnectReason` FIRST, ahead of the breakout auto-return branch (a kick landing mid-breakout must show the removed view, not race a doomed `rejoinMain()` — `banned_at` would just reject the `join` with BZ186), and transitions to a new `'kicked'` `MeetView` via `teardownLiveRoom()` (stops media, nulls `room` — idempotent on the effect's own rerun, no reintroduction of the BZ190-rejoin retry-loop class fixed in `fc7672f8`). **Re-admit affordance:** `meeting_set_role` IS the re-admit path (its own comment: assigning a role to a banned identity clears `banned_at`) — the SAME reducer call the pre-existing "Make co-host" button already wires, so no new backend route. `ControlsBar`'s participants Dialog gained a host/cohost-only "Removed from this meeting" section (new `banned`/`onReAdmit` props) built from `bannedMembers`, a `MeetApp.svelte` `$state<Record<identity,displayName>>` populated live off the `participant_kicked` push (`meeting_get` projects no durable `bannedAt` — a `meeting_read.sql` projection add is the named upgrade seam, ledgered in `TODOS.md`, out of scope for this webmail-only pass) and cleared only on the confirming `participant_role` push (push-is-truth, never optimistic on the Re-admit click itself — the recording/captions/breakouts genre). Re-admit is confirm-free (reversible — a re-kick is one click away), unlike kick's confirm Dialog. **Raw identity leak:** `MeetingRoom.svelte`'s leave a11y announcement used the bare LiveKit identity (`u:1 left the meeting`) instead of resolving `.name` the way the "joined" branch already did — fixed with a small identity→name cache populated every render while a participant is still present, plus a generic `meet.a11y.someone` ("A participant") fallback (never the raw identity) for a participant who never set a display name. Tests: `MeetApp.test.ts` (kicked-view transition incl. the mid-breakout-priority and no-auto-rejoin-loop cases, the banned/re-admit push lifecycle), `ControlsBar.test.ts` (the Removed section's visibility/confirm-free Re-admit), `MeetingRoom.test.ts` (leave announcement name resolution + the no-display-name fallback).
- **B3 Task 6 (store-layer plumbing — Task 7 built the first panel on top):** `$lib/meet/api.ts` gains `Breakout {breakoutId, idx, title, roomName?}` + `openBreakouts`/`assignBreakout`/`closeBreakouts`/`joinBreakout` (the `breakouts.go` wire shapes verbatim); `Meeting` gains `breakoutsEnabled`/`breakouts`/`myBreakoutId`/`captionsEnabled`/`captionsOn` — additive-safe: a private `normalizeMeeting()` defaults all five on every route returning a `Meeting` (`getMeeting`/`listMeetings`/`resolveSlug`/`join`'s nested `meeting`) so a pre-Task-7/13 server response still type-checks AND behaves at runtime, never `undefined`. `$lib/meet/room.svelte.ts`'s `MeetRoom` constructor gains a second arg, `attestedCcIdentity = ''`. The data-channel envelope (`#handleData`) grows three families: `{t:'cc', sid, text, final}` renders into `captionSegments` (a capped ring of 6; interim replaces by `sid`, final always appends) ONLY from the attested identity — an empty `attestedCcIdentity` matches nothing, so captions fail closed (F8); `{t:'wb', elements}` / `{t:'wb_req'}` / `{t:'wb_sync', part, of, seq, elements}` drive the whiteboard — `sendWhiteboard` (unchunked live delta), `requestWhiteboardSync` (asks any peer for the full board), `sendWhiteboardSync` (chunks a full-state answer at `WB_CHUNK_BYTES = 14000` — F15, LiveKit's ~15KiB reliable-data cap — into sequence-numbered parts a receiver reassembles out-of-order-tolerant, discarding an in-flight buffer the moment a newer `seq` supersedes it), `onWhiteboard`/`onWhiteboardRequest` (single-listener subscriptions Task 9's `WhiteboardPanel` wires). `moveTo(token, wsPath)` is the D1 forced-breakout-move choreography — the self-hosted SFU has no `MoveParticipant`, so a move is disconnect-the-old-`Room` + build-and-connect-a-fresh-one with the child token, preserving `localMuted`/`localCameraOff`/`blurEnabled` across the ~1-2s gap (device state is session-scoped); per D4, room-scoped state does NOT survive the move — `captionSegments`, `finalsLog`, and any in-flight `wb_sync` reassembly buffer are cleared, and the new room immediately re-issues `requestWhiteboardSync()` since the whiteboard is per-room, never inherited from the room just left. **B3 retro fix (eng finding #9):** `moveTo` also calls `oldRoom.removeAllListeners()` before reassigning `this.room` (a stray event still in flight on the disconnected old `Room` must never mutate state through a closure that reads `this.room`/`this.*` as if it were live) and resets every other room-scoped field a move could otherwise leak across — `raisedHands`/`reactions`/`dominantSpeakerId`/`connectionQuality`/`disconnectReason`/`screenSharing`.
- **B3 Task 7 (BreakoutsPanel + the forced-move/auto-return choreography — the first B3 panel; Task 8's Q&A tab and Task 9's WhiteboardPanel reuse its side-panel mechanism/main-stage precedent respectively):** SQL step 0 first — `meeting_read.sql`'s `meeting_get` gains `breakoutsEnabled` (the `recordingEnabled` toggle-read genre, `feature.meet_breakouts`), `breakouts[]` (ALWAYS-present anti-oracle array, `{breakoutId, idx, title}` for ANY participant, PLUS a per-row `assignees[]` key — present ONLY on a host/cohost's projection, absent entirely, not merely empty, for a plain member — of `{identity, displayName}`), and `myBreakoutId` (the caller's own assignment, null for a roaming host/cohost per D7). `mailserver/sql/tests/meet_read.pg` grew from 34 to 52 assertions pinning all three.
  - **`BreakoutsPanel.svelte`** (new, host/cohost-only): a count `Select` (1–20, the "stepper"), an `autoAssign` `Switch`, Open → `openBreakouts`; once open, per-assignable-participant `Select`s (D3: `g:` guests and self are filtered client-side — banned/departed participants are never in the LIVE roster `MeetApp` feeds it to begin with, so nothing else needs filtering) calling `assignBreakout`; Close all (in-flight-disabled). Two local-state mirrors reseeded from props on every change (the `LobbyPanel` pattern): `rows` (structural room list) and `localAssignments` (identity→breakoutId, optimistic). `assignments` is fed by `MeetApp`'s own live panel map (`breakoutAssignments`, updated on every `breakout_assigned` push with **zero re-fetch**), not the `breakouts[].assignees` snapshot directly — that field's only consumer is `MeetApp` seeding the map once per load/refetch.
    **B3 retro fix (design finding #4):** `assign()`'s optimistic write is reverted on a genuine failure now, not unconditionally kept. `meetApi.assignBreakout`'s rejection is branched on `MeetError.status`: **5xx** (the eviction-502 case — the reducer's own assignment already committed, only the route's `RemoveParticipant` eviction call failed and the worker heals it) keeps the optimistic write and shows the informational `meet.breakouts.assignFailed` ("saved") copy, unchanged from before; **4xx/network** (a genuine BZ201 anti-oracle rejection, or no response at all) REVERTS `localAssignments[identity]` to its prior value and shows the new `meet.breakouts.assignRejected` ("could not assign — please try again") copy instead.
    **B3 retro fix (design finding #1 — host/cohost roaming):** the panel gained a per-room "Join" button (`onJoinRoom?: (breakoutId: number) => void`, the `onOpenChat` hide-when-undefined idiom — undefined hides every button; the panel is host/cohost-only to begin with, so a plain member never reaches this component) wired in `MeetApp` to the SAME `moveToBreakout` choreography a forced assignment uses, plus a "Return to main" affordance (`onReturnToMain?: () => void`, wired to `rejoinMain`) shown while `selfCurrentBreakoutId !== null` — a new prop mirroring `MeetApp`'s own `currentBreakoutId`, since a host/cohost's server-side `meeting_participants.breakout_id` stays NULL even while roaming (D7) and this client-tracked value is the only place that fact is knowable. Before this fix a host/cohost had NO way to visit a breakout room at all despite the reducer/route layer fully supporting it — stranded alone in main, the core host loop every competitor has. i18n: `meet.breakouts.joinRoom` (reuses `meet.breakouts.mainRoom` for the return affordance), full en/de/zh triads.
  - **`MeetApp.svelte`** — `sidePanel: 'chat' | 'breakouts' | null` replaces the old bare `showChat` boolean (design D6's exclusive side slot — opening one closes the other; `ControlsBar`'s new `onOpenBreakouts`/`breakoutsEnabled` props mirror `onOpenChat`, gated inside the same host/cohost block as the recording button, the `Columns3` icon). `currentBreakoutId` (which room the SELF caller is actually connected to — seeded from the FRESH `join()`/`joinBreakout()` response at every `connectRoom`/`rejoinMain` call, never a stale pre-join snapshot, B3 retro fix eng finding #7/#11) drives the top-anchored banner swap: `meet.breakouts.notRecorded` replaces `RecordingBanner` while non-null (§9, DN4). The move choreography (`moveToBreakout`/`rejoinMain`) shows a `position:fixed;inset:0` interstitial (design D6 "over everything" — video grid, side panels, AND `ControlsBar`, since the caller has no working media during the gap anyway) that takes focus on mount (the B2 D-5 lesson) and restores focus to the room-main region on exit (B3 retro fix design finding #11 — focus used to drop to `<body>`) and is guarded, both for its own transient connectionState blips during `room.moveTo`'s internal teardown+reconnect AND against a meeting-switch racing in mid-move, by the pre-existing `openGeneration` counter (read-only here) plus `moveInterstitial === null`. D1's one-retry is a **plain loop-local counter inside `moveToBreakout`** (`for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 2; attempt++)`), not persisted component state — it cannot outlive a single call, so there is nothing for `teardownLiveRoom` to separately clear for it. `breakout_closed`, the D1 move-failure fallback, an unexpected mid-breakout `Disconnected`, AND a roaming host/cohost's own "Return to main" click all route through the SAME `rejoinMain()`, which uses the plain `join` route (not `joinBreakout`) and re-seeds `recordingActive`/`captionsOn` from ITS OWN response (D3: a member returning into a live recording sees the banner, asserted not assumed). `teardownLiveRoom` additionally clears `sidePanel`/`breakoutAssignments`/`currentBreakoutId`/`moveInterstitial`/`whiteboardOpen` (D4) — `captionSegments`/`finalsLog`/the wb reassembly buffer are `MeetRoom`-internal and die with the room object itself, nothing extra to clear here.
    **B3 retro fixes (post-Task-18 two-reviewer retrospective — HIGH eng finding #1, the D10-redirect bug):** `rejoinMain` previously hard-set `currentBreakoutId = null` unconditionally on success — WRONG whenever the plain `join` route's own D10 redirect (an assignment still live server-side) lands the caller back in the CHILD room instead of main (the D1 move-failure fallback racing a live assignment; a mid-breakout `Disconnected` racing the SAME); the stale `null` un-suppressed captions and dropped the "not recorded" banner while actually still in a child room, AND made a LATER `breakout_closed` push skip its own `currentBreakoutId !== null` rejoin guard, stranding the caller connected to a room the SFU had already deleted. Fixed: `currentBreakoutId = res.meeting.myBreakoutId ?? null` (the fresh join response's own fact — `null` for a genuinely-returned host/cohost roamer, since they carry no server assignment to redirect against). `moveToBreakout`'s D1 fallback now `await`s `rejoinMain()` and only toasts `moveFailed` when the fallback genuinely did NOT land the caller in the target room (`currentBreakoutId !== breakoutId`) — a still-assigned D10 redirect is a real, if belated, success, not a failure to narrate. `rejoinMain`'s catch also treats a `BZ190` (mutation against an already-ended meeting — the ended push racing this exact rejoin) as a silent no-op rather than an error screen (LOW eng finding #12): the `meeting_state:ended` handler owns that transition. **B3 confirm-pass fix (eng NEW-1):** that same `BZ190` catch now ALSO clears `currentBreakoutId` — without it, an SFU eviction landing before the `meeting_state:ended` push left the disconnect `$effect`'s guard (`currentBreakoutId !== null`) intact, re-firing `rejoinMain` every time the catch cleared `moveInterstitial` and looping one `POST /join` per iteration until the (loss-tolerant) ended push arrived. **Design finding #3 (whiteboard scene crossing rooms):** both `moveToBreakout` and `rejoinMain` now set `whiteboardOpen = false` on entry — an open board previously stayed mounted across a move and answered the NEW room's `wb_req` with the OLD room's scene. **Design finding #9 (interstitial label race):** `moveToBreakout`'s room-name lookup can race the `breakout_opened` refetch on the COMMON auto-assign path; `moveInterstitial.roomLabel` is now nullable and the template falls back to the generic `meet.breakouts.movingGeneric` copy instead of a numberless "Breakout " label. **Design finding #10 (foreign-meeting guard order):** the `breakout_closed` push handler now checks `evt.meetingId === meeting.meetingId` BEFORE clearing `breakoutAssignments`, not after — a push for a meeting the caller isn't even looking at previously wiped a DIFFERENT, still-rendered meeting's panel map.
  - **Deviation from the plan's literal file list (documented, not silent):** the interstitial and the "not recorded" banner line were implemented in `MeetApp.svelte`, not `MeetingRoom.svelte`. Rationale: the recording banner they replace already lives in `MeetApp`'s own `.meet-app__banner` slot (never inside `MeetingRoom`), and D6 requires the interstitial to cover `ControlsBar` and the side panels too — surfaces `MeetingRoom` (deliberately a dumb, plain-props video-grid renderer, "trivially testable with plain fakes") has no reach into. `MeetingRoom.svelte` itself is unchanged by Task 7.
  - **The guest join page** (`routes/meet/join/[slug]/+page.svelte`) — `ControlsBar`'s new required `breakoutsEnabled` prop is wired `false` (guests are never assignable, D7). Its `MeetRoom` construction site originally omitted the `cc:{meetingId}` attested-captions identity (the public guest-lobby wire surface carried no numeric meeting id) — **dissolved by a B3 retro fix**, see the Task 15 captions entry below.
  - i18n: full en/de/zh triads for all 15 `meet.breakouts.*` keys (`meet-keys.test.ts`'s golden covers key-parity + the referenced-but-missing direction).
- **B3 Task 8 (Poll button + Q&A tab — spec §4.2's verified zero-backend verdict: polls already function in-meeting today via the bridged `chat_channels` row; this ships only the entry point + a client-side filter, webmail-only, no Go/SQL change):**
  - **Poll button (`ControlsBar.svelte`)**: a new `onOpenPoll?: () => void` prop, same visibility rule as the pre-existing `onOpenChat` (undefined whenever the chat panel isn't mountable — `feature.chat` OFF ⇒ no panel ⇒ no button) and deliberately NOT host-gated (poll creation carries no role check, A3 Task 15). The pass-through chain: `ControlsBar`'s Poll `IconButton` calls `onOpenPoll` → `MeetApp.svelte`'s `openPoll()` sets `sidePanel = 'chat'`, `await tick()`, then calls `chatPanelRef.openPollDialog()` (a `bind:this` ref on `MeetChatPanel`, the Task 7 `sidePanel`-slot precedent) → `MeetChatPanel.svelte`'s exported `openPollDialog()` sets its own `activeTab = 'chat'` (the Q&A tab unmounts `Composer`), `await tick()`, then calls `composerRef.openPollDialog()` (a `bind:this` ref on `Composer`) → `Composer.svelte`'s `openPollDialog()` — **exported** (previously a private function; the ONLY change to Composer itself) so this is the SAME poll-creation dialog the "+" menu already opens, never a duplicate. Two `tick()`s are load-bearing: the outer one mounts `MeetChatPanel` (conditional on `sidePanel==='chat'`), the inner one mounts `Composer` (conditional on `activeTab==='chat'`) — each is invisible to its caller, so the pass-through composes cleanly without either layer knowing the other's internal mount gating.
  - **Q&A tab (`MeetChatPanel.svelte`)**: a `Tabs` (M1 primitive) header switches between Chat and Q&A — Q&A is a pure client-side filter over the SAME `chatStore.messagesByChannel[channelId]` array `MessageList` already reads (spec §4.2's "zero new message plumbing" — no new store field). A question is an ordinary chat message (`msgType==='question'`, Task 3's route allowlist); the list is `msgType==='question' && parentMessageId==null && deletedAt==null`, sorted by the existing `reactions` 👍 count desc, tie-broken by `seq` asc. "Ask a question" is a real `<form onsubmit=...>` (D6 composition rule — native submit/Enter, not a div+button) calling `chatApi.sendMessage(channelId, body, null, [], null, [], 'question')` directly (NOT `chatStore.send`, which has no `msgType` param) — the sent row arrives back through the existing WS `ChatEvent`→`chatStore.applyEvent` path this panel already subscribes to. Upvote is the existing `chatStore.toggleReaction(channelId, messageId, '👍')` — no new reducer, no new BZ.
  - **D9 "Answered" badge** — derived CLIENT-SIDE from `chatApi.threadMessages(parentId)`, fetched ONLY when a question row is expanded (never eagerly for every row in the list — that would be a fetch per question to paint a badge nobody may look at), never a new server flag. **The host/cohost signal**: there is no dedicated participant-roster-with-meeting-role read route client-side (the same accepted gap `MeetApp.svelte`'s `sheetParticipants` ponytail comment documents — cosmetic role badges only, real authority is enforced server-side regardless). The already-fetched chat-channel `roster` (`RosterMember.role`) is used as the proxy instead: `chat_channel_create` seeds the meeting's creator (the meeting host) as channel role `'owner'` (`chat_channel.sql`), and `meeting_join`'s plain `INSERT INTO chat_channel_members (channel_id, user_id)` never role-bumps anyone else — so `role IN ('owner','admin')` on the reply's author is an accurate host/cohost signal without a new route. A cohost who is never independently promoted to a chat-channel role would not trip this proxy — a documented, accepted narrowing (ponytail: add a real meeting-role roster route if this narrowing ever surfaces as a real gap, same upgrade seam the `sheetParticipants` comment names).
  - i18n: `meet.controls.poll` / `meet.qa.tab` / `meet.qa.ask` / `meet.qa.answered` / `meet.qa.empty`, full en/de/zh triads.
  - Tests: `ControlsBar.test.ts` (Poll button visibility + click), `MeetChatPanel.test.ts` (Q&A filter/sort, the real-`<form>` submit, the on-expand Answered derivation for both a plain-member and an owner-role reply, upvote), `Composer.test.ts`/`MeetApp.test.ts` unaffected (existing suites stay green — `openPollDialog`'s export is additive). No MeetApp-level end-to-end test drives the full four-hop click chain through a real LiveKit-mocked in-room render (would need `$lib/chat/api` mocked into `MeetApp.test.ts` too, a heavier harness than the risk warrants for four lines of type-checked delegation) — each hop's own contract is unit-tested instead, and `npm run check`'s 0-error pass across the whole `bind:this` chain is the wiring proof.
- **B3 Task 9 (whiteboard — Excalidraw as a lazy React chunk, client-only, no Go/SQL/toggle at all, DN6):**
  - **`$lib/meet/whiteboard.ts`** is the ONE file in the entire app that imports React/`@excalidraw/excalidraw` — every `.svelte` file stays React-free (`WhiteboardPanel.svelte` reaches it only via `await import('$lib/meet/whiteboard')` inside `onMount`, never a static import; guarded by a source-text test, not just a behavioral one). No JSX (`React.createElement` only — no build-plugin change). `mountWhiteboard(el, {theme, onChange, onExport?, onFull?}) → {applyRemote, getSceneElements, setTheme, unmount}`. Protocol logic is split into plain, React-free functions so it stays unit-testable despite `@excalidraw/excalidraw` itself being unimportable in jsdom (it feature-detects canvas support via `getContext('2d')` at MODULE-IMPORT time, and jsdom has no canvas without the optional `canvas` npm package — `whiteboard.test.ts` mocks the package entirely, the `blur.ts`/`@livekit/track-processors` genre): `isOverCap`/`WB_ELEMENT_CAP=1000` (F15 — a local edit that would push the scene over the cap is dropped from the broadcast, never sent, and reports once via `onFull`; an over-cap REMOTE payload is defensively truncated before merge, no `onFull`), `diffChangedElements`/`versionsOf` (broadcasts only elements whose `version` moved since the last send — Excalidraw's `onChange` fires on every pointer-move, so a naive full-resend would flood Task 6's `wb` channel), `wireRoomSync(room, handle)` (wires `room.onWhiteboard→handle.applyRemote`, `room.onWhiteboardRequest→room.sendWhiteboardSync(handle.getSceneElements())`, and fires `room.requestWhiteboardSync()` once on open — the late-joiner sync request — against a structurally-typed `RoomWhiteboardOps`, zero compile-time dependency on `room.svelte.ts` itself). `applyRemote` uses the package's REAL exported `reconcileElements` (version/versionNonce LWW, `@excalidraw/excalidraw`'s own collab algorithm — confirmed exported at pin time, so no reimplementation was needed) via `api.updateScene({elements, captureUpdate: CaptureUpdateAction.NEVER})` — `NEVER` keeps a remote-applied merge out of the LOCAL undo history. "Export to chat": `exportToBlob` renders the current scene to a PNG `Blob`, handed out via `onExport` to a button whiteboard.ts itself renders INSIDE Excalidraw's own `renderTopRightUI` chrome (design D9 — the ONE sanctioned exception to token-only styling anywhere in this app, since that surface is Excalidraw's own rendered DOM, not ours; everything OUTSIDE the canvas — `WhiteboardPanel.svelte`'s header/loading state — stays token-styled like every other Meet surface). `onExport` is `undefined` (button doesn't render at all) whenever there's no bridged chat channel.
  - **`WhiteboardPanel.svelte`** (new): owns the Svelte-side wiring only — the loading state (`meet.wb.loading`) while the dynamic import resolves; `exportToChat(blob)` (opens the chat channel via `chatStore.openChannel` only if it isn't already the active one, `chatStore.addAttachments(channelId, null, [pngFile])`, then `chatStore.send('', null)` — the SAME A2 chunked-upload rail Composer's file-picker uses, an attachment-only message the same shape `sendVoice` already established, so the PNG inherits Drive governance/retention for free); theme sync via a `MutationObserver` on `document.documentElement`'s `data-theme` attribute (no reactive theme store exists app-wide — theme lives on a DOM attribute applied imperatively by `$lib/theme/apply.ts` — a `MutationObserver` is the lazy way to notice a LIVE toggle without inventing a new store for this one consumer); `onDestroy` unmounts the React root AND unregisters the room's wb listeners (`room.onWhiteboard(null)` / `room.onWhiteboardRequest(null)`, a B3 retro fix, design finding #9-nit — without this, a `wb`/`wb_req` frame arriving after the panel unmounts called into the dead Excalidraw handle right up until, if ever, a future `WhiteboardPanel` re-mount overwrote the listeners; `room.svelte.ts`'s `onWhiteboard`/`onWhiteboardRequest` both now accept `null` to unregister).
  - **Entry point + composition (design D2/D6, `ControlsBar.svelte`/`MeetApp.svelte`)**: `ControlsBar`'s new `onToggleWhiteboard?: () => void` prop is unconditional for every authed member (no server toggle to gate — DN6 — and never role-gated, the Poll-button precedent: collaborative by nature) but follows the `onOpenChat`/`onOpenPoll` hide-when-undefined idiom SOLELY because of spec §11's guest exclusion (whiteboard, like breakouts/polls/Q&A, defers to the guest-accounts program) — the public guest join page (`routes/meet/join/[slug]/+page.svelte`) simply omits the prop, so the button never renders there. `MeetApp.svelte`'s `whiteboardOpen` is a MAIN-STAGE boolean, deliberately NOT part of the `sidePanel` exclusive-slot state (design D6: a 320px canvas is useless, and the whiteboard can be open AT THE SAME TIME as chat/breakouts — different screen region entirely). Opening it mounts `WhiteboardPanel` above the video region and collapses that region to a fixed-height (96px) strip via CSS only — `MeetingRoom.svelte` itself is UNCHANGED and never remounted (no track/tile state lost toggling the whiteboard) — `ponytail: the collapsed strip reuses MeetingRoom's own gallery grid at a fixed height rather than a dedicated horizontal filmstrip layout; a real filmstrip is a MeetingRoom.svelte prop change, outside this task's scope — upgrade if the squeeze ever reads as broken rather than just small.` Joins the existing D4 `teardownLiveRoom` enumeration (a meeting switch mid-whiteboard leaks nothing into the next meeting).
  - **Self-hosted assets, no CDN**: `static/excalidraw/fonts/` (~13MB, committed — the `static/fonts/` precedent for this repo's on-prem posture) is copied VERBATIM from the pinned package's own `node_modules/@excalidraw/excalidraw/dist/prod/fonts/` (every family, including the `Xiaolai` CJK fallback the package's own `FONT_FAMILY_FALLBACKS` auto-substitutes for non-Latin glyphs — dropping it would silently degrade Chinese text on the canvas for this zh-mandatory product); `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH = '/excalidraw/'` set once inside `mountWhiteboard` (never the package's default CDN).
  - **Integrity pin (XIAOLAI-001)**: the verbatim copy is enforced, not just prose — `excalidraw-fonts.json` (written by `scripts/vendor-excalidraw-fonts.mjs`) is the authoritative integrity manifest (per-file `bytes`+`sha256` for every shard, including all 209 `Xiaolai/*` shards; the 32-hex filename suffixes are upstream labels, not content hashes), and `src/lib/theme/font-shards.test.ts` fails on any shard modified, added without a manifest entry, or removed, and on byte drift from the installed pinned package. The one exception is the manifest `replacements` list: `Liberation/LiberationSans-Regular.woff2` is a modified Latin subset of the OFL-1.1 Liberation Sans 2.1.5, renamed "Setjoin Sans Fallback" in its name table (the OFL Reserved Font Name; the package ships GPL-2.0 1.05; notice in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`); the vendor script keeps it and the drift test skips it.
  - **Dependency pinning (deliberate exception to this repo's usual caret-range convention)**: `react`/`react-dom`/`@excalidraw/excalidraw` are pinned EXACT (no `^`/`~`) in `package.json` — mirrors the `lib-livekit.sh` pinned-binary posture elsewhere in this program (self-hosted, on-prem, no surprise transitive bump on an embedded third-party UI toolkit).
  - **Build-pipeline fix (fix bugs before continuing, not a silent workaround)**: `@excalidraw/excalidraw`'s `index.css` package-export is gated behind custom `development`/`production` conditions with no `default` fallback; this project's `vite.config.js` had hard-coded `resolve.conditions` to `['browser']` only, which satisfies neither, so `vite build` (rolldown) failed to resolve the CSS import. Fixed by making `vite.config.js` mode-aware (`defineConfig(({mode}) => ({...}))`, appending `mode==='production'?'production':'development'` to the conditions list — restoring the automatic behavior the override had been suppressing for every OTHER package too, not just this one). A second, test-ONLY fix: `open-color` (an `@excalidraw/excalidraw` transitive dependency) ships a bare `main:"open-color.json"` CJS package with no import assertion, which Node's native ESM loader — Vitest's default path for externalized deps — rejects; `vite.config.js`'s `test.server.deps.inline` now routes `@excalidraw/excalidraw` + `open-color` through Vite's own transform instead (the real `vite build` client path never hits this, since it never externalizes to Node's loader).
  - **DN5 chunk-size verification (build-artifact proof, not just asserted)**: a before/after `vite build` diff (git-stashing Task 9's files, rebuilding, then restoring) showed the app's shared entry/root-layout chunks (`entry/app.js`, `entry/start.js`, root layout nodes 0/1) changing by single-digit bytes (minifier/hash noise, unrelated to this change) while the `/meet` route's own already-separate chunk grew ~1KB gz for the new button/panel-wrapper/state glue — the Excalidraw+React payload itself (~830KB gz total across ~55 of Excalidraw's own internally-code-split chunks, headlined by the ~230KB gz entry chunk `mountWhiteboard` lives in — in the ballpark the plan's "~1MB gz" estimate named) is reachable ONLY through the dynamic `import('$lib/meet/whiteboard')`: zero references to any of those chunk hashes exist anywhere in the app's entry/start/root-layout files.
  - **CSP verdict: NONE (verified, not assumed)** — build-artifact static analysis (grepped the built Excalidraw-tagged chunks) found zero `eval`/`new Function`/`WebAssembly` usage (so no `script-src` delta, unlike the B1 background-blur WASM path); `font-src 'self'` (already present) covers the self-hosted `/excalidraw/fonts/` path; the only external hostnames bundled (Excalidraw's own help-menu links) are user-initiated anchor navigations, never a runtime `connect-src` fetch. No triple-lock CSP process was needed.
  - i18n: `meet.wb.open` / `meet.wb.loading` / `meet.wb.exportToChat` / `meet.wb.full` / `meet.wb.ephemeral`, full en/de/zh triads.
  - Tests: `whiteboard.test.ts` (cap/diff/reconcile-wiring/export-button logic against a mocked `@excalidraw/excalidraw`), `WhiteboardPanel.test.ts` (lazy-load sequencing incl. a source-text no-eager-import guard, loading state, the chat-export glue, teardown — `$lib/meet/whiteboard` mocked wholesale), `ControlsBar.test.ts`/`MeetApp.test.ts` (button visibility, main-stage toggle, teardown) with `WhiteboardPanel.svelte` itself stubbed to `() => null` (the `OrgChart.svelte` mocking precedent) — no test anywhere in the suite attempts a real canvas render.
- **B3 Task 15 (captions UI — toggle + server-state chip, guest-visible DN3 + attested overlay F8 + always-mounted finals-only live region D7):** wires webmail up to the now-code-complete `asrbot`/`CaptionsController` producer (Task 14) — Tasks 12–14 had already shipped the server side; this task is the whole client consumer.
  - **Bug fixed first (fix bugs before continuing):** `room.svelte.ts`'s `cc` envelope parsing never read the wire's `p` field (asrbot's `ccPayload`, `asrbot/internal/bot/session.go` — the speaking participant's identity) and stamped `CaptionSegment.identity` from the DATA-CHANNEL SENDER instead (always the bot's own `cc:{meetingId}` identity) — harmless while Task 6 shipped dormant with no per-speaker consumer, wrong the instant this task needed real attribution. Fixed at the one parse site (`CaptionEnvelope` gains `p: string`; `#addCaption` records `msg.p`, not `participant.identity`; the F8 sender check is unchanged). Commit `10505fee`.
  - **`CaptionsOverlay.svelte`** (new) is the WHOLE client consumer in one component — mounted UNCONDITIONALLY from `MeetingRoom.svelte` (member AND guest surfaces alike, since both mount `MeetingRoom` the same way): an ALWAYS-mounted `role="log"` region, a small chip (`meet.captions.chip`/`meet.captions.unavailable`), and a bottom-anchored 2-line styled transcript that dedupes `segments` to ONE entry per `sid` (latest write wins — an interim replace-in-place keeps one array entry per sid, and a later final for the same sid is a newer, later entry, so "last write per sid" naturally supersedes the interim ghost without `room.svelte.ts`'s ring needing to change) with per-speaker attribution via a `participantNames: Record<string,string>` prop `MeetingRoom.svelte` builds from the SAME `Participant` objects it already renders tiles from. The transcript hides (chip + sr-only region stay) while `hideTranscript` is true — `MeetApp` passes `whiteboardOpen`, a B3 retro fix (design finding #10) for the transcript bleeding over the 96px-tall video filmstrip the whiteboard collapses the gallery to.
  - **D7 append-only region model (B3 retro fix, eng finding #13 / design D7-break — the original model, superseded):** the region used to derive `finalSegments` from `segments.filter(s => s.final)` (the SAME small 6-entry visual ring the styled transcript reads) and key `{#each finalSegments as seg, i (i)}` by array INDEX. Both were bugs: an unrelated INTERIM append can evict an old FINAL from the 6-entry ring (a final "leaving" is a region mutation triggered by an interim frame, never a final itself), and the index key meant every window shift rewrote every remaining row's text — a screen reader re-announcement flood, not the append-only region D7 promises. Fixed with two changes: `room.svelte.ts` gained `finalsLog` — a SEPARATE, much-larger (`FINALS_LOG_CAP = 50`) array that `#addCaption` appends a final to and NEVER evicts on an interim (the small `captionSegments` ring is untouched, still feeding the styled transcript exactly as before); `CaptionsOverlay` reads the region from `finalsLog`, keyed by `seg.sid` (a stable per-utterance identity from the ASR engine) instead of array index. A new final now inserts exactly one new region node; every existing node's text is untouched by ring turnover elsewhere.
  - **Degrade/fade timer (design D8), ONE `$effect` per instance:** `captionsOn` is server truth (never local optimism); once true, a 15s window with no NEW attested frame flips `degraded`. The effect depends on the `segments` ARRAY REFERENCE (not `.length`) — `room.svelte.ts` reassigns a fresh array on every `#addCaption` call, append OR in-place interim replace alike, so this catches both, not just new sids (pinned: a same-sid interim text update resets the timer). **B3 retro fix (design finding #5a — the heartbeat frame):** asrbot publishes a `{t:'cc', hb:true}` heartbeat every 5s on the SAME data channel as real cc frames — no `sid`/`text`/`p`/`final`, so it never touches `segments` and the OLD effect (keyed only on the `segments` reference) would eventually degrade a genuinely-healthy session with no talkers. `room.svelte.ts` gained `lastAttestedCcAt` (an epoch-ms `$state`, bumped on EVERY attested `cc` frame including the heartbeat, never on a spoofed sender); the degrade effect now also depends on it, threaded through `MeetingRoom`'s new `captionsLastAttestedAt` prop. `fadeOnDegrade` (default `false`, members/host/cohost) keeps the chip visible with the `meet.captions.unavailable` status line once degraded (they have a push rail that will correct a real crash); `fadeOnDegrade={true}` (the guest join page) hides chip+transcript entirely once degraded instead — D8's actual "fade to off," since a guest has no push rail to ever self-correct a stale "still on" claim.
  - **THE GUEST-PAGE CEILING IS DISSOLVED (B3 retro fix, design findings #4/#5 — was "THE GUEST-PAGE REALITY"):** `meeting_lobby.sql`'s admitted-poll envelope now projects `ccIdentity` (`'cc:' || meetingId`) on BOTH admitted arms (guest `g:` and member-knock `u:`), unconditional on `captionsOn` (landed in the backend wave this webmail pass builds on). `LobbyWaiting.svelte`'s `onAdmitted` widened to a 4th argument carrying it; the guest join page (`routes/meet/join/[slug]/+page.svelte`) seeds `guestCcIdentity` from it and constructs `MeetRoom(choice.codec, guestCcIdentity)` — the SAME real attestation path a member gets. The old ceiling (fails closed to `attestedCcIdentity=''`, `segments` structurally always `[]`, chip always fades on a fixed 15s timer with no way to self-correct) no longer applies: a guest with live frames behaves identically to a member (real frame belt in, chip only fades once frames AND heartbeats genuinely stop). The member-knock caller (`MeetApp.handleKnockAdmitted`) ignores the same 4th argument — it already derives `cc:{meetingId}` from its own live `meeting.meetingId` state, which a knocking member always has and a guest never does.
  - **`ControlsBar.svelte`**: a captions toggle IconButton, visible only when `captionsEnabled && captionsConfigured && isHostOrCohost` (nested in the SAME host/cohost block as breakouts/recording) — `captionsEnabled` is `meeting.captionsEnabled` (Task 13's toggle-read), `captionsConfigured` is Task 14's join-envelope-only signal (whether the ASR sidecar pair is actually deployed). Click toggles by the CURRENT `captionsOn` (never local optimism — the label/icon only flips once the server's own `captions_state` push lands); a local `captionsStartBusy` disables the button for the in-flight START call only (Stop is idempotent server-side, D8, guarded instead by `MeetApp`'s own `captionsStopBusy`, the `stopBusy` genre).
  - **`MeetApp.svelte`**: `captionsOn`/`captionsConfigured` are `MeetStore`'s own `$state` (arch review #A12), seeded from the join/knock-admit envelope's `meeting.captionsOn`/`res.captionsConfigured` and flipped thereafter ONLY by the `captions_state` push (an ordinary all-live-members fan-out, the `breakout_closed` genre — every member renders the identical chip, unlike `breakout_assigned`'s targeted-by-payload shape). No F2a-style "push wins" machinery — captions carries none of recording's consent/legal weight, so a rare join-vs-push race is an accepted, simpler gap (ponytail, named inline). `doStartCaptions` catches `MeetError.code==='captionsBusy'` for its own distinguishable toast (the `egressBusy` genre); `doStopCaptions` mirrors `doStopRecording`'s `stopBusy` double-DELETE guard. Both reset in `teardownLiveRoom` (D4 enumeration). Design D4 breakout suppression is caller-side, not `MeetingRoom`'s concern: `MeetingRoom`'s `captionsOn`/`captionSegments` props are passed `false`/`[]` while `currentBreakoutId !== null` (the overlay/chip literally never know about breakouts), and `rejoinMain` re-seeds `captionsOn` from its own fresh `join()` read on return to main — the same catch-up-read posture `recordingActive` already uses for the SAME return-to-main call.
  - **`MeetingRoom.svelte`**: mounts `CaptionsOverlay` inside a NEW `.meeting-room__bottom-stack` flex-column wrapper it shares with the existing connection-degraded banner (design D6 "Meet composition" — bottom-anchored, captions above the banner, `gap` alone keeps them from ever overlapping regardless of which is showing; no manual offset math). `captionsOn`/`captionSegments`/`captionsFadeOnDegrade` are optional props (default `false`/`[]`/`false`); B3 retro fixes added `captionsFinalsLog` (default `[]`, the D7 append-only region source above), `captionsHideTranscript` (default `false`, design finding #10), and `captionsLastAttestedAt` (default `0`, design finding #5a) — this component still knows nothing about breakouts or the guest-vs-member distinction; all are the caller's job.
  - **2026-07-16 delta (B3b live-gate P0 1b): the bot joins UNHIDDEN — `room.svelte.ts`'s `#syncParticipants` filters `cc:`-prefixed identities out of `participants`.** LiveKit strips a hidden participant's data-packet sender identity, which was silently killing F8 attestation (every `cc` frame dropped, captions rendered nowhere); `mailserver/internal/meet/AGENTS.md`'s Captions section carries the full A/B-proof narrative. The filter is the ONE choke point every consumer above (`MeetingRoom`'s gallery tiles/`participantNames` map, `ControlsBar`'s participants sheet via `MeetApp`'s `sheetParticipants`/`hasGuestParticipant`, the guest join page's own `sheetParticipants`) reads through — none of them needed their own filter. `#handleData`'s F8 attestation check is UNCHANGED and unaffected (it reads the `RoomEvent.DataReceived` callback's sender directly, never `participants`).
  - i18n: `meet.captions.on` / `meet.captions.off` / `meet.captions.chip` / `meet.captions.busy` / `meet.captions.unavailable`, full en/de/zh triads (pinned by `meet-keys.test.ts`'s key-parity + referenced-in-source golden checks).
  - Tests: `CaptionsOverlay.test.ts` (new — the always-mounted/finals-only region, interim-replace/final-persist dedup, per-speaker attribution, the degrade/fade timer incl. a frame-arrival reset and the member-vs-guest `fadeOnDegrade` split), `room.svelte.test.ts` (the `p`-field bugfix + a missing-`p`-is-dropped case), `MeetingRoom.test.ts` (the CaptionsOverlay mount + participantNames map), `ControlsBar.test.ts` (the 3-signal visibility gate, start/stop wiring, in-flight disable), `MeetApp.test.ts` (join/knock-admit seeding, the `captions_state` push arm incl. cross-meeting isolation, the captionsBusy toast, the stopBusy double-click guard, teardown reset), `LobbyWaiting.test.ts` (the widened 3-arg `onAdmitted`, incl. `captionsOn:true`).
- **In-room mobile collapse (whole-pipeline review UX #9).** `.meet-app__side` (the shared wrapper `MeetChatPanel`/`BreakoutsPanel`/`LobbyPanel` all render inside) gained the SAME `@media (max-width: 767px) { position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 30; }` overlay rule `MeetChatPanel`'s own `.meet-chat` already carried — below that, whichever panel is open covers the full viewport instead of docking at a fixed 320px beside the video (which crushed the room-main video column to a sliver on phone-width viewports for ALL THREE panels, not just chat, since only chat had its own escape-hatch CSS). **`LobbyPanel` joined the `sidePanel` exclusive slot** (`'chat' | 'breakouts' | 'lobby' | null`) instead of being unconditionally docked whenever `myRole` was host/cohost — a real behavior change on desktop too (previously always visible; now toggled via a new host/cohost-only "Waiting to join" `ControlsBar` button carrying a pending-count badge), needed because the fixed-overlay CSS above would otherwise have made it a PERMANENT full-screen overlay hiding the video for every mobile host. `LobbyPanel` gained an `onClose` prop + header close button (the `MeetChatPanel` precedent — a full-screen overlay needs its own dismiss affordance). **Bug found + fixed in the same pass**: the `join_request_created` push handler's `inThatRoom` skip ("LobbyPanel inside the room is the surface — never both") already didn't refresh `meeting.pendingJoinRequests` on that event even before this change — a host already in-room silently never saw a live knock arrive, in the panel OR via toast, until they left/reloaded. Fixed as part of the same handler rewrite this toggle needed anyway: it now always refetches `meeting` when in the SAME room (so opening the panel shows the fresh row) and only suppresses the toast when the panel is actually open (`sidePanel==='lobby'`); the toast's action button opens the panel directly (`sidePanel='lobby'`) instead of a same-room no-op page nav. Tests: `LobbyPanel.test.ts` (close button presence/click), `ControlsBar.test.ts` (button visibility/gating, badge), `MeetApp.test.ts` (toggle open/close, the toast-vs-refresh branching for both panel states).

- **MEET plan M3 — document presentation UX (all behind `feature.meet_document_presentation` AND `feature.drive`, the meeting_get `presentationEnabled` projection; default OFF hides every surface).** Components under `src/lib/components/meet/`: `PresentationPicker.svelte` (Drive picker dialog reusing the authorized `listSpaces`/`tree`/`listVersions` APIs — space/file/version → prepare → PRIVATE preview with the fidelity-warning banner → Present; supported launch modes pdf/ppt/pptx/odp/dps filtered client-side; empty/retry/stale-selection/prep-cancel/named-failure states), `PresentationStage.svelte` (the main-stage canvas: presenter + attendee layouts, prev/next + ArrowLeft/Right scoped to the focusable stage region, LOCAL zoom (announced local, never shared), reading view (explicit limitation when a page has no qualified accessible representation), ephemeral pointer/ink overlay (SVG, normalized coords, visibly temporary per R5.5 — cleared on page/source transitions, suspend, tool-off), stale banner + resync (last validated page stays visible, never inferred ended), DEGRADED banner, handoff dialog + authorized takeover, screen-share fallback button), `PresentationRail.svelte` (presenter-only thumbnails/notes rail; notes fetch is presenter-gated client-side AND server-side, bottom-sheet under 1024px). State: `$lib/meet/presentationStore.svelte.ts` (`PresentationStore`, fresh per MeetStore) — the M0 law in code: state promotes ONLY from API responses (the authorized checkpoint read / a mutation's committed fact); data-channel frames and `presentation_state` pushes are hints fed to the frozen `CheckpointRefresher`; `setPresentationContext` is fed exclusively via M2's `presentationFrameContextFromCheckpoint` (terminal ⇒ null context, fail closed) and reset on every teardown (`teardownLiveRoom` → `presentation.reset()`, the D4 genre). Transport: `$lib/meet/presentationApi.ts` — typed wrappers over the LANDED M2 routes (`internal/meet/presentation.go`: prepare/ready/start/page/presenter/end + the checkpoint read; `amPresenter`/`myParticipantId` are SERVER truth) plus the `getManifest` seam for M1's authorized meet-pages-v1 serving (absent on the wire today: the manifest read fails VISIBLY with the generic failure copy + fallback — never fabricated pages; `prepare` pins the manifest digest per M2's body contract). Pure logic: `$lib/meet/presentationClient.ts` (zoom clamp, page bounds incl. `MAX_PAGE_INDEX`, prefetch plan (current+2), pointer TTL/throttle, warning/failure vocabularies→literal i18n keys, throttle-error normalization). Room plane: `room.svelte.ts` gained the EPHEMERAL `t:'stage'` family (`StagePointerEvent`/`StageInkEvent`/`StageInkClearEvent`, per-sender monotonic `seq` dedup, `MAX_STAGE_FRAME_BYTES=2048` send cap, shape-validated receive, watermarks cleared in `moveTo`) — deliberately disjoint from the frozen `t:'presentation'` durable family so an ephemeral frame can never impersonate a durable kind. Integration: `MeetApp.svelte` (whiteboard and document are mutually exclusive MAIN-STAGE modes — opening the whiteboard suspends the stage (`suspend()` clears ephemeral ink, keeps durable truth), closing re-syncs (reloads the checkpoint); the video region collapses to the whiteboard-genre strip so captions keep flowing (the permanent sr-only log persists); breakout moves suspend (no parent-room capability; the room swap nulls the frame context); the connect effect calls `sync()` on every (re)connect — returning participants REAUTHORIZE, never reuse cached grants); `ControlsBar.svelte` gained only the additive `onPresentDocument` entry (hidden when undefined = flag OFF / live session / breakout; CSS-hidden at phone widths — V1 file selection needs a larger viewport; every existing control stays). Known M2-gap honesty: presenter GRANTS target `meeting_attendee` participant ids, which the webmail roster (LiveKit identity/name only) cannot map — the handoff dialog shows its explanatory empty state until a participant-id-bearing roster projection lands; takeover (self-target, server-fenced) works without it. Tests: `presentationStore.svelte.test.ts` (promotion-only-from-responses, hint coalescing + throttle backoff, presenter gate, grant/takeover/end fences, stage-plane TTL/cap/clear, teardown fail-closed), `presentationClient.test.ts` (pure helpers + frozen vocabularies), `room.stage.test.ts` (stage family shape/seq/cap/room-swap), `PresentationStage.test.ts`/`PresentationPicker.test.ts` (the named interaction states), i18n `meet.presentation.*` (en/de/zh).

- **MEET-DP1 — presentation session lifetime:** the existing `PresentationStore` captures the original Meeting identity and retires superseded asynchronous work on cancellation, replacement, suspension and teardown. Checkpoint/manifest/text/mutation success, rejection and finally paths cannot promote departed work; source/page/presenter text checks and monotone checkpoint promotion preserve current authorized state. The store owns the frozen refresher's injected timers (cancelled on retirement) and rendition polling timers. A late accepted preparation drains through the existing authorized end transport using its ORIGINAL Meeting/presentation IDs; no credentials are retained. One attempt runs per cancellation or explicit retry, duplicate attempts coalesce, and failures survive session reset separately from current presentation state. `MeetApp` renders the existing persistent Retry toast for those retained cleanup failures; retry uses current server authorization and success removes the record. The room effect suspends while whiteboard/breakout mode is active and reauthorizes on connected main-stage return even without an existing live session; local checkpoint promotion does not retrigger that effect. Picker dismissal cancels initial pending preparation and PREPARING/READY work, while preserving an already ACTIVE session. An authorized ACTIVE checkpoint finishes preview immediately even when the start reply is still pending; an older reply cannot replace the checkpoint or announce stale state. Controlled deferred transport and actual dialog Close/Escape tests cover cancellation, reordered results, old errors/finally, timer teardown and original-identity retry; they do not qualify the still-absent manifest route or admit rollout. Reducer, frozen-envelope and transport contracts remain required.

---

### Apps launcher (`src/routes/apps/+page.svelte`)

- Grid launcher for all platform apps; respects per-user effective-access list.
- Redirects to first enabled app if user has app entitlements (not hardcoded `/inbox`).
- Tiles are generated from `$lib/nav/navConfig`'s `RAIL_APPS` (the SAME list/order/icons the rail, drawer, and command palette use) filtered by `effectiveCaps.enabledApps` — never a hand-duplicated app list, so a new rail app (drive/chat/meet/approvals, all four once missing here — a real bug fixed 2026-07-18) can't silently go missing from the launcher again. The Admin tile stays a separate, hardcoded entry gated on `$user?.isAdmin` (admin isn't a `RAIL_APPS`/`AppId` member — the rail exposes it as `RAIL_ADMIN` sub-consoles instead).

---

### Downloads (`src/routes/downloads/+page.svelte`)

- Desktop client download page; reads version cache from `/downloads` endpoint.

Lists the caller's own rules with an enable/disable `Switch` (PUT) + edit/delete (DELETE, `confirm()`-gated) and a human-readable condition/action summary rendered as TEXT (never `{@html}`). Create/edit form is a condition builder (field/op/value rows) + action builder (type + single param row) mirroring the exact `rule_engine.sql` vocab — fields `from_addr`/`subject`/`body`/`spam_score`/`size_bytes`/`has_attachments`/`sender_domain`, ops `contains`/`not_contains`/`equals`/`not_equals`/`starts_with`/`gt`/`lt`/`gt_bytes`/`lt_bytes`/`is_true`/`matches_regex`/`in_list`, actions `move_to`/`flag`/`mark_read`/`categorize`/`delete`/`forward_to`/`add_header` (param key per type: move_to→folder, categorize→category, forward_to→address, add_header→value). Conditions are saved as a flat AND list of simple `{field,op,value}` objects (the engine's implicit-AND array form); the engine's OR/NOT/group nesting is not exposed by the builder — grouped rules created elsewhere degrade to an empty condition list on edit. Linked from the Mail card on `/settings`. i18n under `rules.*`.
